Input

{
  "filePath": "/source/standards/karpathy-guidelines.mdc"
}

Output

<path>/source/standards/karpathy-guidelines.mdc</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Use when writing, reviewing, or refactoring code to avoid overcomplication, make surgical changes, surface assumptions, and define verifiable success criteria.
3: alwaysApply: true
4: ---
5: 
6: # Karpathy behavioral guidelines
7: 
8: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Merge with project-specific instructions as needed.
9: 
10: **Tradeoff:** These guidelines bias toward caution over speed. For trivial tasks, use judgment.
11: 
12: ## 1. Think Before Coding
13: 
14: **Don't assume. Don't hide confusion. Surface tradeoffs.**
15: 
16: Before implementing:
17: 
18: - State your assumptions explicitly. If uncertain, ask.
19: - If multiple interpretations exist, present them - don't pick silently.
20: - If a simpler approach exists, say so. Push back when warranted.
21: - If something is unclear, stop. Name what's confusing. Ask.
22: 
23: ## 2. Simplicity First
24: 
25: **Minimum code that solves the problem. Nothing speculative.**
26: 
27: - No features beyond what was asked.
28: - No abstractions for single-use code.
29: - No "flexibility" or "configurability" that wasn't requested.
30: - No error handling for impossible scenarios.
31: - If you write 200 lines and it could be 50, rewrite it.
32: 
33: Ask yourself: "Would a senior engineer say this is overcomplicated?" If yes, simplify.
34: 
35: ## 3. Surgical Changes
36: 
37: **Touch only what you must. Clean up only your own mess.**
38: 
39: When editing existing code:
40: 
41: - Don't "improve" adjacent code, comments, or formatting.
42: - Don't refactor things that aren't broken.
43: - Match existing style, even if you'd do it differently.
44: - If you notice unrelated dead code, mention it - don't delete it.
45: 
46: When your changes create orphans:
47: 
48: - Remove imports/variables/functions that YOUR changes made unused.
49: - Don't remove pre-existing dead code unless asked.
50: 
51: The test: Every changed line should trace directly to the user's request.
52: 
53: ## 4. Goal-Driven Execution
54: 
55: **Define success criteria. Loop until verified.**
56: 
57: Transform tasks into verifiable goals:
58: 
59: - "Add validation" → "Write tests for invalid inputs, then make them pass"
60: - "Fix the bug" → "Write a test that reproduces it, then make it pass"
61: - "Refactor X" → "Ensure tests pass before and after"
62: 
63: For multi-step tasks, state a brief plan:
64: 
65: ```
66: 1. [Step] → verify: [check]
67: 2. [Step] → verify: [check]
68: 3. [Step] → verify: [check]
69: ```
70: 
71: Strong success criteria let you loop independently. Weak criteria ("make it work") require constant clarification.
72: 
73: ---
74: 
75: **These guidelines are working if:** fewer unnecessary changes in diffs, fewer rewrites due to overcomplication, and clarifying questions come before implementation rather than after mistakes.

(End of file - total 75 lines)
</content>

read · completed

Input

{
  "filePath": "/source/standards/ponytail.mdc"
}

Output

<path>/source/standards/ponytail.mdc</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode. Always pick the simplest solution that works.
3: globs:
4: alwaysApply: true
5: ---
6: 
7: # Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode
8: 
9: You are a lazy senior developer. Lazy means efficient, not careless. The best code is the code never written.
10: 
11: Before writing any code, stop at the first rung that holds:
12: 
13: 1. Does this need to be built at all? (YAGNI)
14: 2. Does it already exist in this codebase? Reuse the helper, util, or pattern that's already here, don't re-write it.
15: 3. Does the standard library already do this? Use it.
16: 4. Does a native platform feature cover it? Use it.
17: 5. Does an already-installed dependency solve it? Use it.
18: 6. Can this be one line? Make it one line.
19: 7. Only then: write the minimum code that works.
20: 
21: The ladder runs after you understand the problem, not instead of it: read the task and the code it touches, trace the real flow end to end, then climb.
22: 
23: Bug fix = root cause, not symptom: a report names a symptom. Grep every caller of the function you touch and fix the shared function once — one guard there is a smaller diff than one per caller, and patching only the path the ticket names leaves a sibling caller still broken.
24: 
25: Rules:
26: 
27: - No abstractions that weren't explicitly requested.
28: - No new dependency if it can be avoided.
29: - No boilerplate nobody asked for.
30: - Deletion over addition. Boring over clever. Fewest files possible.
31: - Shortest working diff wins, but only once you understand the problem. The smallest change in the wrong place isn't lazy, it's a second bug.
32: - Question complex requests: "Do you actually need X, or does Y cover it?"
33: - Pick the edge-case-correct option when two stdlib approaches are the same size, lazy means less code, not the flimsier algorithm.
34: - Mark deliberate simplifications that cut a real corner with a known ceiling (global lock, O(n²) scan, naive heuristic) with a `ponytail:` comment naming the ceiling and upgrade path.
35: 
36: Not lazy about: understanding the problem (read it fully and trace the real flow before picking a rung, a small diff you don't understand is just laziness dressed up as efficiency), input validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs (the platform is never the spec ideal, a clock drifts, a sensor reads off), anything explicitly requested. Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures). Trivial one-liners need no test.

(End of file - total 36 lines)
</content>

Trivial one-liners need no test.\n\n(End of file - total 36 lines)\n</content>",

        "text": "---\ndescription: Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode. Always pick the simplest solution that works.\nglobs:\nalwaysApply: true\n---\n\n# Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode\n\nYou are a lazy senior developer. Lazy means efficient, not careless. The best code is the code never written.\n\nBefore writing any code, stop at the first rung that holds:\n\n1. Does this need to be built at all? (YAGNI)\n2. Does it already exist in this codebase? Reuse the helper, util, or pattern that's already here, don't re-write it.\n3. Does the standard library already do this? Use it.\n4. Does a native platform feature cover it? Use it.\n5. Does an already-installed dependency solve it? Use it.\n6. Can this be one line? Make it one line.\n7. Only then: write the minimum code that works.\n\nThe ladder runs after you understand the problem, not instead of it: read the task and the code it touches, trace the real flow end to end, then climb.\n\nBug fix = root cause, not symptom: a report names a symptom. Grep every caller of the function you touch and fix the shared function once — one guard there is a smaller diff than one per caller, and patching only the path the ticket names leaves a sibling caller still broken.\n\nRules:\n\n- No abstractions that weren't explicitly requested.\n- No new dependency if it can be avoided.\n- No boilerplate nobody asked for.\n- Deletion over addition. Boring over clever. Fewest files possible.\n- Shortest working diff wins, but only once you understand the problem. The smallest change in the wrong place isn't lazy, it's a second bug.\n- Question complex requests: \"Do you actually need X, or does Y cover it?\"\n- Pick the edge-case-correct option when two stdlib approaches are the same size, lazy means less code, not the flimsier algorithm.\n- Mark deliberate simplifications that cut a real corner with a known ceiling (global lock, O(n²) scan, naive heuristic) with a `ponytail:` comment naming the ceiling and upgrade path.\n\nNot lazy about: understanding the problem (read it fully and trace the real flow before picking a rung, a small diff you don't understand is just laziness dressed up as efficiency), input validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs (the platform is never the spec ideal, a clock drifts, a sensor reads off), anything explicitly requested. Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures). Trivial one-liners need no test.",

345: @@ -65,10 +65,20 @@ The data is provided in the context of a container that belongs to a containerla
346:  
347:  The `inspect` command produces a brief summary about the running lab components. It is also possible to get a full view on the running containers by adding `--details` flag.
348:  
349:  With this flag inspect command will output every bit of information about the running containers. This is what `docker inspect` command provides.
350:  
351: +#### containers
352: +
353: +By default `inspect` shows one entry per topology node.
354: +
355: +Nodes that consist of several containers (ie. distributed SR-SIM) are represented by a single node that carries the management addresses of the node.
356: +
357: +The local flag `--containers` will list every container of a lab rather than just the logical nodes.
358: +
359: +When the lab is selected with the `--topo` flag, only the containers of the nodes defined in that topology file are listed.
360: +
361:  #### wide
362:  
363:  The local `-w | --wide` flag adds all available columns to the `inspect` output table.
364:  
365:  ### Examples
366: diff --git a/docs/manual/kinds/srl.md b/docs/manual/kinds/srl.md
367: index 269d4ba..3aac436 100644
368: --- a/docs/manual/kinds/srl.md
369: +++ b/docs/manual/kinds/srl.md
370: @@ -468,11 +468,11 @@ The license file lifts these limitations as well as unlocks chassis-based platfo
371:  
372:  ### Reboot
373:  
374:  A reboot issued from within the node, for example with `tools platform chassis reboot`, restarts the SR Linux container. The node comes back with its links, saved configuration and management address intact.
375:  
376: -To support that, SR Linux nodes default to the `unless-stopped` [restart policy](../nodes.md#restart-policy), and containerlab keeps the links and the management address of each node in an internal `<node-name>-netns` container. The internal container is only listed by `containerlab inspect --all`, and it is removed together with the node.
377: +To support that, SR Linux nodes default to the `unless-stopped` [restart policy](../nodes.md#restart-policy), and containerlab keeps the links and the management address of each node in an internal `<node-name>-netns` container. The internal container is only listed by `containerlab inspect` with the [`--containers`](../../cmd/inspect/index.md#containers) or `--details` flag, and it is removed together with the node.
378:  
379:  Use `containerlab stop`, `start` and `restart` to manage the lifecycle of SR Linux nodes instead of restarting the containers with the container runtime directly.
380:  
381:  Nodes with a user-defined [`network-mode`](../nodes.md#network-mode) do not use the internal container.

111: func inspectFn(cobraCmd *cobra.Command, o *Options) error {
112: 	if o.Global.TopologyName == "" && o.Global.TopologyFile == "" && !o.Destroy.All {
113: 		return fmt.Errorf(
114: 			"provide either a lab name (--name) or a topology file path (--topo) or the --all flag",
115: 		)
116: 	}
117: 
118: 	// Format validation (only relevant if --details is NOT used)
119: 	if !o.Inspect.Details &&
120: 		o.Inspect.Format != clabconstants.FormatTable &&
121: 		o.Inspect.Format != clabconstants.FormatJSON &&
122: 		o.Inspect.Format != clabconstants.FormatCSV {
123: 		return fmt.Errorf(
124: 			"output format %q is not supported when --details is not used, use "+
125: 				"'table', 'json' or 'csv'",
126: 			o.Inspect.Format,
127: 		)
128: 	}
129: 	// If --details is used, the format is implicitly JSON.
130: 	if o.Inspect.Details {
131: 		o.Inspect.Format = clabconstants.FormatJSON // Force JSON format if details are requested
132: 	}
133: 
134: 	c, err := clabcore.NewContainerLab(o.ToClabOptions()...)
135: 	if err != nil {
136: 		return err
137: 	}
138: 
139: 	containers, err := listContainers(cobraCmd.Context(), c, o)
140: 	if err != nil {
141: 		return err
142: 	}
143: 
144: 	// Handle empty results
145: 	if len(containers) == 0 {
146: 		switch o.Inspect.Format {
147: 		case clabconstants.FormatJSON:
148: 			fmt.Println("{}")
149: 		case clabconstants.FormatCSV:
150: 			fmt.Println(
151: 				"lab_name,labPath,absLabPath,name,container_id,image,kind,state,status," +
152: 					"ipv4_address,ipv6_address,owner",
153: 			)
154: 		default: // Table format
155: 			log.Info("no containers found")
156: 		}
157: 
158: 		return err
159: 	}
160: 
161: 	// Handle --details (always produces grouped JSON output)
162: 	if o.Inspect.Details {
163: 		return printContainerDetailsJSON(containers)
164: 	}
165: 
166: 	// Handle non-details cases (table or grouped JSON summary)
167: 	err = PrintContainerInspect(containers, o)
168: 
169: 	return err
170: }

172: // listContainers handles listing containers based on different criteria (topology or labels).
173: func listContainers(
174: 	ctx context.Context,
175: 	c *clabcore.CLab,
176: 	o *Options,
177: ) ([]clabruntime.GenericContainer, error) {
178: 	if c.LabRuntime != nil {
179: 		return c.ListLabRuntimeContainers(ctx, o.Destroy.All)
180: 	}
181: 
182: 	var listOptions []clabcore.ListOption
183: 
184: 	switch {
185: 	case o.Global.TopologyFile != "":
186: 		listOptions = append(listOptions, clabcore.WithListLabName(c.Config.Name))
187: 	case o.Global.TopologyName != "":
188: 		listOptions = append(listOptions, clabcore.WithListLabName(o.Global.TopologyName))
189: 	default:
190: 		listOptions = append(listOptions, clabcore.WithListclabLabelExists())
191: 	}
192: 
193: 	containers, err := c.ListContainers(ctx, listOptions...)
194: 	if err != nil {
195: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to list containers based on labels: %s", err)
196: 	}
197: 
198: 	if o.Global.TopologyFile != "" {
199: 		containers = slices.DeleteFunc(containers, func(ctr clabruntime.GenericContainer) bool {
200: 			_, isNode := c.Nodes[ctr.Labels[clabconstants.NodeName]]
201: 			_, isComponent := c.Nodes[ctr.Labels[clabconstants.RootNodeName]]
202: 
203: 			return !isNode && !isComponent
204: 		})
205: 
206: 		// containers that containerlab did not create (ie. ext-container) have no lab labels.
207: 		nodeContainers, err := c.ListNodesContainersIgnoreNotFound(ctx)
208: 		if err != nil {
209: 			return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to list containers based on topology: %s", err)
210: 		}
211: 
212: 		for idx := range nodeContainers {
213: 			if !slices.ContainsFunc(containers, func(ctr clabruntime.GenericContainer) bool {
214: 				return ctr.ID == nodeContainers[idx].ID
215: 			}) {
216: 				containers = append(containers, nodeContainers[idx])
217: 			}
218: 		}
219: 	}
220: 
221: 	// --details always describes every container of a lab.
222: 	if o.Inspect.Containers || o.Inspect.Details {
223: 		return containers, nil
224: 	}
225: 
226: 	return logicalNodeContainers(containers), nil
227: }
228: 
229: // logicalNodeContainers returns a single logical topology node.
230: func logicalNodeContainers(
231: 	containers []clabruntime.GenericContainer,
232: ) []clabruntime.GenericContainer {
233: 	// Node names are unique only within a lab.
234: 	type nodeKey struct{ lab, node string }
235: 
236: 	holders := make(map[nodeKey]*clabruntime.GenericContainer)
237: 	hasCPMA := make(map[nodeKey]bool)
238: 
239: 	for idx := range containers {
240: 		l := containers[idx].Labels
241: 		key := nodeKey{l[clabconstants.Containerlab], l[clabconstants.RootNodeName]}
242: 
243: 		switch {
244: 		case key.node == "":
245: 		case l[clabconstants.InternalNode] == "true":
246: 			holders[key] = &containers[idx]
247: 		case l[clabconstants.NodeName] == key.node+"-a":
248: 			hasCPMA[key] = true
249: 		}
250: 	}
251: 
252: 	result := make([]clabruntime.GenericContainer, 0, len(containers))
253: 
254: 	for idx := range containers {
255: 		ctr := containers[idx]
256: 		name := ctr.Labels[clabconstants.NodeName]
257: 		key := nodeKey{
258: 			ctr.Labels[clabconstants.Containerlab],
259: 			ctr.Labels[clabconstants.RootNodeName],
260: 		}
261: 
262: 		// Components are named <root node>-<lowercase slot>.
263: 		isCPM := name == key.node+"-a" || (name == key.node+"-b" && !hasCPMA[key])
264: 		if ctr.Labels[clabconstants.ToolType] != "" ||
265: 			ctr.Labels[clabconstants.InternalNode] == "true" || (key.node != "" && !isCPM) {
266: 			continue
267: 		}
268: 
269: 		if key.node == "" {
270: 			key.node = name
271: 		}
272: 
273: 		if holder := holders[key]; holder != nil {
274: 			ctr.NetworkName = holder.NetworkName
275: 			ctr.NetworkSettings = holder.NetworkSettings
276: 			ctr.Ports = holder.Ports
277: 		}
278: 
279: 		result = append(result, ctr)
280: 	}
281: 
282: 	return result
283: }
284: 
285: func toTableData(contDetails []clabtypes.ContainerDetails, o *Options) []tableWriter.Row {
286: 	tabData := make([]tableWriter.Row, 0, len(contDetails))
287: 
288: 	for i := range contDetails {
289: 		d := &contDetails[i]
290: 		tabRow := tableWriter.Row{}
291: 
292: 		if o.Destroy.All {
293: 			tabRow = append(tabRow, d.LabPath, d.LabName)
294: 		}
295: 
296: 		if o.Inspect.Wide {
297: 			tabRow = append(tabRow, d.Owner)
298: 		}
299: 
300: 		// we do not want to print status other than health in the table view
301: 		if !strings.Contains(d.Status, "health") {
302: 			d.Status = ""
303: 		} else {
304: 			d.Status = fmt.Sprintf("(%s)", d.Status)
305: 		}
306: 
307: 		// Common fields
308: 		if o.Inspect.Wide {
309: 			// Print all fields on one line, no newlines
310: 			tabRow = append(tabRow,
311: 				d.Name,
312: 				fmt.Sprintf("%s %s", d.Kind, d.Image),
313: 				fmt.Sprintf("%s %s", d.State, d.Status),
314: 				fmt.Sprintf("%s %s",
315: 					ipWithoutPrefix(d.IPv4Address),
316: 					ipWithoutPrefix(d.IPv6Address)))
317: 		} else {
318: 			tabRow = append(tabRow,
319: 				d.Name,
320: 				fmt.Sprintf("%s\n%s", d.Kind, d.Image),
321: 				fmt.Sprintf("%s\n%s", d.State, d.Status),
322: 				fmt.Sprintf("%s\n%s",
323: 					ipWithoutPrefix(d.IPv4Address),
324: 					ipWithoutPrefix(d.IPv6Address)))
325: 		}
326: 
327: 		tabData = append(tabData, tabRow)
328: 	}
329: 
330: 	return tabData
331: }

333: // getShortestTopologyPath calculates the relative path to the provided topology file from
334: // the current working directory and returns it if it is shorted than the absolute path p.
335: func getShortestTopologyPath(p string) (string, error) {
336: 	if p == "" {
337: 		return "", nil
338: 	}
339: 	if strings.Contains(p, "://") {
340: 		return p, nil
341: 	}
342: 
343: 	// get topo file path relative of the cwd
344: 	cwd, err := os.Getwd()
345: 	if err != nil {
346: 		return "", err
347: 	}
348: 
349: 	relPath, err := filepath.Rel(cwd, p)
350: 	if err != nil {
351: 		return "", err
352: 	}
353: 
354: 	if len(relPath) < len(p) {
355: 		return relPath, nil
356: 	}
357: 
358: 	return p, nil
359: }

361: // printContainerDetailsJSON handles the detailed JSON output of containers grouped by lab name.
362: func printContainerDetailsJSON(containers []clabruntime.GenericContainer) error {
363: 	groupedDetails := make(map[string][]clabruntime.GenericContainer)
364: 	// Sort containers first by lab name, then by container name for consistent output
365: 	sort.Slice(containers, func(i, j int) bool {
366: 		labNameI := containers[i].Labels[clabconstants.Containerlab]
367: 		labNameJ := containers[j].Labels[clabconstants.Containerlab]
368: 
369: 		if labNameI == labNameJ {
370: 			// Use the first name if available
371: 			nameI := ""
372: 			if len(containers[i].Names) > 0 {
373: 				nameI = containers[i].Names[0]
374: 			}
375: 
376: 			nameJ := ""
377: 			if len(containers[j].Names) > 0 {
378: 				nameJ = containers[j].Names[0]
379: 			}
380: 
381: 			return nameI < nameJ
382: 		}
383: 
384: 		return labNameI < labNameJ
385: 	})
386: 
387: 	// Group the sorted containers
388: 	for idx := range containers {
389: 		labName := containers[idx].Labels[clabconstants.Containerlab]
390: 		// Ensure labName exists, default to a placeholder if missing
391: 		// (shouldn't happen with filters)
392: 		if labName == "" {
393: 			labName = "_unknown_lab_"
394: 		}
395: 
396: 		groupedDetails[labName] = append(groupedDetails[labName], containers[idx])
397: 	}
398: 
399: 	b, err := json.MarshalIndent(groupedDetails, "", "  ")
400: 	if err != nil {
401: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to marshal grouped container details: %v", err)
402: 	}
403: 
404: 	fmt.Println(string(b))
405: 
406: 	return nil
407: }
408: 
409: func printContainerInspectJSON(contDetails []clabtypes.ContainerDetails) error {
410: 	// Group summary results by LabName
411: 	// Use a map where keys are lab names and values are slices of container details
412: 	groupedLabs := make(map[string][]clabtypes.ContainerDetails)
413: 
414: 	for idx := range contDetails {
415: 		labName := contDetails[idx].LabName
416: 		if labName == "" {
417: 			labName = "_unknown_lab_" // Should not happen if filters work correctly
418: 		}
419: 		// Assign the *entire* ContainerDetails struct (including AbsLabPath)
420: 		groupedLabs[labName] = append(groupedLabs[labName], contDetails[idx])
421: 	}
422: 
423: 	b, err := json.MarshalIndent(groupedLabs, "", "  ")
424: 	if err != nil {
425: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to marshal grouped container summary: %v", err)
426: 	}
427: 
428: 	fmt.Println(string(b))
429: 
430: 	return nil
431: }

433: func printContainerInspectTable(contDetails []clabtypes.ContainerDetails, o *Options) {
434: 	// Generate and render table using the summary data (which uses relative LabPath)
435: 	tabData := toTableData(contDetails, o)
436: 	table := tableWriter.NewWriter()
437: 	table.SetOutputMirror(os.Stdout)
438: 	table.SetStyle(tableWriter.StyleRounded)
439: 	table.Style().Format.Header = text.FormatTitle
440: 	table.Style().Format.HeaderAlign = text.AlignCenter
441: 	table.Style().Options.SeparateRows = true
442: 	table.Style().Color = tableWriter.ColorOptions{
443: 		Header: text.Colors{text.Bold},
444: 	}
445: 
446: 	headerBase := tableWriter.Row{"Name", "Kind/Image", "State", "IPv4/6 Address"}

448: 		headerBase = slices.Insert(headerBase, 0, "Owner")
449: 	}

451: 	var header tableWriter.Row
452:

458: 		colConfigs = append(
459: 			colConfigs,

466: 				AutoMerge: true, VAlign: text.VAlignMiddle,
467: 			},

470: 			colConfigs = append(colConfigs, tableWriter.ColumnConfig{
471: 				Number:    3, //nolint: mnd
472: 				AutoMerge: true, VAlign: text.VAlignMiddle,
473: 			})
474: 		}
475: 	} else {
476: 		header = headerBase
477: 
478: 		colConfigs = append(colConfigs, tableWriter.ColumnConfig{
479: 			Number:    1,
480: 			AutoMerge: true, VAlign: text.VAlignMiddle,
481: 		})
482: 	}
483: 
484: 	table.AppendHeader(header)
485: 
486: 	if len(colConfigs) > 0 {
487: 		table.SetColumnConfigs(colConfigs)
488: 	}
489: 
490: 	table.AppendRows(tabData)
491: 	table.Render()
492: }

494: func printContainerInspectCSV(contDetails []clabtypes.ContainerDetails) {
495: 	csv := "lab_name,labPath,absLabPath,name,container_id,image,kind,state," +
496: 		"status,ipv4_address,ipv6_address,owner\n"
497: 	for idx := range contDetails {
498: 		csv += fmt.Sprintf("%v,%v,%v,%v,%v,%v,%v,%v,%v,%v,%v,%v\n",
499: 			contDetails[idx].LabName,
500: 			contDetails[idx].LabPath,
501: 			contDetails[idx].AbsLabPath,
502: 			contDetails[idx].Name,
503: 			contDetails[idx].ContainerID,
504: 			contDetails[idx].Image,
505: 			contDetails[idx].Kind,
506: 			contDetails[idx].State,
507: 			contDetails[idx].Status,
508: 			contDetails[idx].IPv4Address,
509: 			contDetails[idx].IPv6Address,
510: 			contDetails[idx].Owner)
511: 	}
512: 
513: 	fmt.Print(csv)
514: }

516: // PrintContainerInspect handles non-details output (table or grouped JSON summary).
517: func PrintContainerInspect(containers []clabruntime.GenericContainer, o *Options) error {
518: 	contDetails := make([]clabtypes.ContainerDetails, 0, len(containers))
519: 
520: 	// Gather summary details of each container
521: 	for idx := range containers {
522: 		absPath := containers[idx].Labels[clabconstants.TopoFile]
523: 
524: 		shortPath, err := getShortestTopologyPath(absPath)
525: 		if err != nil {
526: 			log.Warnf(
527: 				"failed to get relative topology path for container %s: %v, using raw path %q",
528: 				containers[idx].Names[0],
529: 				err,
530: 				absPath,
531: 			)
532: 
533: 			shortPath = absPath // Use raw path as fallback for display
534: 		}
535: 
536: 		status := parseStatus(containers[idx].Status)
537: 
538: 		cdet := clabtypes.ContainerDetails{
539: 			LabName:     containers[idx].Labels[clabconstants.Containerlab],
540: 			LabPath:     shortPath, // Relative or shortest path for table view
541: 			AbsLabPath:  absPath,   // Absolute path for JSON view
542: 			Image:       containers[idx].Image,
543: 			State:       containers[idx].State,
544: 			Status:      status,
545: 			IPv4Address: containers[idx].GetContainerIPv4(),
546: 			IPv6Address: containers[idx].GetContainerIPv6(),
547: 			ContainerID: containers[idx].ShortID,
548: 			// Only lab runtimes set the workload; it adds namespace, pod, container, node_uid,
549: 			// exposed_ports and load_balancer_address to the JSON output.
550: 			LabRuntimeWorkload: containers[idx].Workload,
551: 		}
552: 
553: 		if len(containers[idx].Names) > 0 {
554: 			cdet.Name = containers[idx].Names[0]
555: 		}
556: 
557: 		if group, ok := containers[idx].Labels[clabconstants.NodeGroup]; ok {
558: 			cdet.Group = group
559: 		}
560: 
561: 		if kind, ok := containers[idx].Labels[clabconstants.NodeKind]; ok {
562: 			cdet.Kind = kind
563: 		}
564: 
565: 		if owner, ok := containers[idx].Labels[clabconstants.Owner]; ok {
566: 			cdet.Owner = owner
567: 		}
568: 
569: 		contDetails = append(contDetails, cdet)
570: 	}
571: 
572: 	// Sort summary details by lab name, then container name
573: 	sort.Slice(contDetails, func(i, j int) bool {
574: 		if contDetails[i].LabName == contDetails[j].LabName {
575: 			return contDetails[i].Name < contDetails[j].Name
576: 		}
577: 
578: 		return contDetails[i].LabName < contDetails[j].LabName
579: 	})
580: 
581: 	switch o.Inspect.Format {
582: 	case clabconstants.FormatJSON:
583: 		err := printContainerInspectJSON(contDetails)
584: 		if err != nil {
585: 			return err
586: 		}
587: 	case "table":
588: 		printContainerInspectTable(contDetails, o)
589: 	case "csv":
590: 		printContainerInspectCSV(contDetails)
591: 	default:
592: 		return fmt.Errorf("internal error: unhandled format %q", o.Inspect.Format)
593: 	}
594: 
595: 	return nil
596: }
597: 
598: // parseStatus extracts a simpler status string, focusing on health states.
599: func parseStatus(status string) string {
600: 	switch {
601: 	case strings.Contains(status, "unhealthy"):
602: 		return "unhealthy"
603: 	case strings.Contains(status, "health: starting"):
604: 		return "health: starting"
605: 	case strings.Contains(status, "healthy"):
606: 		return "healthy"
607: 	default:
608: 		// Return original status if no specific health info found
609: 		return status
610: 	}
611: }
612: 
613: // ipWithoutPrefix removes the CIDR prefix length from an IP address string.
614: // Returns "N/A" if input contains "N/A".
615: // Returns original string if it doesn't contain exactly one "/".
616: func ipWithoutPrefix(ip string) string {
617: 	if strings.Contains(ip, clabconstants.NotApplicable) {
618: 		return ip
619: 	}
620: 
621: 	ipParts := strings.Split(ip, "/")
622: 	if len(ipParts) != 2 { //nolint: mnd
623: 		return ip // Return original if not in expected format "address/prefix"
624: 	}
625: 
626: 	return ipParts[0]
627: }

either a lab name (--name) or a topology file path (--topo) or the --all flag\",\n\t\t)\n\t}\n\n\t// Format validation (only relevant if --details is NOT used)\n\tif !o.Inspect.Details &&\n\t\to.Inspect.Format != clabconstants.FormatTable &&\n\t\to.Inspect.Format != clabconstants.FormatJSON &&\n\t\to.Inspect.Format != clabconstants.FormatCSV {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\n\t\t\t\"output format %q is not supported when --details is not used, use \"+\n\t\t\t\t\"'table', 'json' or 'csv'\",\n\t\t\to.Inspect.Format,\n\t\t)\n\t}\n\t// If --details is used, the format is implicitly JSON.\n\tif o.Inspect.Details {\n\t\to.Inspect.Format = clabconstants.FormatJSON // Force JSON format if details are requested\n\t}\n\n\tc, err := clabcore.NewContainerLab(o.ToClabOptions()...)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\n\tcontainers, err := listContainers(cobraCmd.Context(), c, o)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\n\t// Handle empty results\n\tif len(containers) == 0 {\n\t\tswitch o.Inspect.Format {\n\t\tcase clabconstants.FormatJSON:\n\t\t\tfmt.Println(\"{}\")\n\t\tcase clabconstants.FormatCSV:\n\t\t\tfmt.Println(\n\t\t\t\t\"lab_name,labPath,absLabPath,name,container_id,image,kind,state,status,\" +\n\t\t\t\t\t\"ipv4_address,ipv6_address,owner\",\n\t\t\t)\n\t\tdefault: // Table format\n\t\t\tlog.Info(\"no containers found\")\n\t\t}\n\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\n\t// Handle --details (always produces grouped JSON output)\n\tif o.Inspect.Details {\n\t\treturn printContainerDetailsJSON(containers)\n\t}\n\n\t// Handle non-details cases (table or grouped JSON summary)\n\terr = PrintContainerInspect(containers, o)\n\n\treturn err\n}\n\n// listContainers handles listing containers based on different criteria (topology or labels).\nfunc listContainers(\n\tctx context.Context,\n\tc *clabcore.CLab,\n\to *Options,\n) ([]clabruntime.GenericContainer, error) {\n\tif c.LabRuntime != nil {\n\t\treturn c.ListLabRuntimeContainers(ctx, o.Destroy.All)\n\t}\n\n\tvar listOptions []clabcore.ListOption\n\n\tswitch {\n\tcase o.Global.TopologyFile != \"\":\n\t\tlistOptions = append(listOptions, clabcore.WithListLabName(c.Config.Name))\n\tcase o.Global.TopologyName != \"\":\n\t\tlistOptions = append(listOptions, clabcore.WithListLabName(o.Global.TopologyName))\n\tdefault:\n\t\tlistOptions = append(listOptions, clabcore.WithListclabLabelExists())\n\t}\n\n\tcontainers, err := c.ListContainers(ctx, listOptions...)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"failed to list containers based on labels: %s\", err)\n\t}\n\n\tif o.Global.TopologyFile != \"\" {\n\t\tcontainers = slices.DeleteFunc(containers, func(ctr clabruntime.GenericContainer) bool {\n\t\t\t_, isNode := c.Nodes[ctr.Labels[clabconstants.NodeName]]\n\t\t\t_, isComponent := c.Nodes[ctr.Labels[clabconstants.RootNodeName]]\n\n\t\t\treturn !isNode && !isComponent\n\t\t})\n\n\t\t// containers that containerlab did not create (ie. ext-container) have no lab labels.\n\t\tnodeContainers, err := c.ListNodesContainersIgnoreNotFound(ctx)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"failed to o.Inspect.Containers || o.Inspect.Details {\n\t\treturn containers, nil\n\t}\n\n\treturn logicalNodeContainers(containers), nil\n}\n\n// logicalNodeContainers returns a single logical topology node.\nfunc logicalNodeContainers(\n\tcontainers []clabruntime.GenericContainer,\n) []clabruntime.GenericContainer {\n\t// Node names are unique only within a lab.\n\ttype nodeKey struct{ lab, node string }\n\n\tholders := make(map[nodeKey]*clabruntime.GenericContainer)\n\thasCPMA := make(map[nodeKey]bool)\n\n\tfor idx := range containers {\n\t\tl := containers[idx].Labels\n\t\tkey := nodeKey{l[clabconstants.Containerlab], l[clabconstants.RootNodeName]}\n\n\t\tswitch {\n\t\tcase key.node == \"\":\n\t\tcase l[clabconstants.InternalNode] == \"true\":\n\t\t\tholders[key] = &containers[idx]\n\t\tcase l[clabconstants.NodeName] == key.node+\"-a\":\n\t\t\thasCPMA[key] = true\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tresult := make([]clabruntime.GenericContainer, 0, len(containers))\n\n\tfor idx := range containers {\n\t\tctr := containers[idx]\n\t\tname := ctr.Labels[clabconstants.NodeName]\n\t\tkey := nodeKey{\n\t\t\tctr.Labels[clabconstants.Containerlab],\n\t\t\tctr.Labels[clabconstants.RootNodeName],\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Components are named <root node>-<lowercase slot>.\n\t\tisCPM := name == key.node+\"-a\" || (name == key.node+\"-b\" && !hasCPMA[key])\n\t\tif ctr.Labels[clabconstants.ToolType] != \"\" ||\n\t\t\tctr.Labels[clabconstants.InternalNode] == \"true\" || (key.node != \"\" && !isCPM) {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tif key.node == \"\" {\n\t\t\tkey.node = name\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tif holder := holders[key]; holder != nil {\n\t\t\tctr.NetworkName = holder.NetworkName\n\t\t\tctr.NetworkSettings = holder.NetworkSettings\n\t\t\tctr.Ports = holder.Ports\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tresult = append(result, ctr)\n\t}\n\n\treturn result\n}\n\nfunc toTableData(contDetails []clabtypes.ContainerDetails, o *Options) []tableWriter.Row {\n\ttabData := make([]tableWriter.Row, 0, len(contDetails))\n\n\tfor i := range contDetails {\n\t\td := &contDetails[i]\n\t\ttabRow := tableWriter.Row{}\n\n\t\tif o.Destroy.All {\n\t\t\ttabRow = append(tabRow, d.LabPath, d.LabName)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tif o.Inspect.Wide {\n\t\t\ttabRow = append(tabRow, d.Owner)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// we do not want to print status other than health in the table view\n\t\tif !strings.Contains(d.Status, \"health\") {\n\t\t\td.Status = \"\"\n\t\t} else {\n\t\t\td.Status = fmt.Sprintf(\"(%s)\", d.Status)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Common fields\n\t\tif o.Inspect.Wide {\n\t\t\t// Print all fields on one line, no newlines\n\t\t\ttabRow = append(tabRow,\n\t\t\t\td.Name,\n\t\t\t\tfmt.Sprintf(\"%s %s\", d.Kind, d.Image),\n\t\t\t\tfmt.Sprintf(\"%s %s\", d.State, d.Status),\n\t\t\t\tfmt.Sprintf(\"%s %s\",\n\t\t\t\t\tipWithoutPrefix(d.IPv4Address),\n\t\t\t\t\tipWithoutPrefix(d.IPv6Address)))\n\t\t} else {\n\t\t\ttabRow = append(tabRow,\n\t\t\t\td.Name,\n\t\t\t\tfmt.Sprintf(\"%s\\n%s\", d.Kind, d.Image),\n\t\t\t\tfmt.Sprintf(\"%s\\n%s\", d.State, d.Status),\n\t\t\t\tfmt.Sprintf(\"%s\\n%s\",\n\t\t\t\t\tipWithoutPrefix(d.IPv4Address),\n\t\t\t\t\tipWithoutPrefix(d.IPv6Address)))\n\t\t}\n\n\t\ttabData = append(tabData, tabRow)\n\t}\n\n\treturn tabData\n}\n\n// getShortestTopologyPath calculates the relative path to the provided topology file from\n// the current working directory and returns it if it is shorted than the absolute path p.\nfunc getShortestTopologyPath(p string) (string, error) {\n\tif p == \"\" {\n\t\treturn \"\", nil\n\t}\n\tif strings.Contains(p, \"://\") {\n\t\treturn p, nil\n\t}\n\n\t// get topo file path relative of the cwd\n\tcwd, err := os.Getwd()\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn \"\", err\n\t}\n\n\trelPath, err := filepath.Rel(cwd, p)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn \"\", err\n\t}\n\n\tif len(relPath) < len(p) {\n\t\treturn relPath, nil\n\t}\n\n\treturn p, nil\n}\n\n// printContainerDetailsJSON handles the detailed JSON output of containers grouped by lab name.\nfunc printContainerDetailsJSON(containers []clabruntime.GenericContainer) error {\n\tgroupedDetails := make(map[string][]clabruntime.GenericContainer)\n\t// Sort containers first by lab name, then by container name for consistent output\n\tsort.Slice(containers, func(i, j int) bool {\n\t\tlabNameI := containers[i].Labels[clabconstants.Containerlab]\n\t\tlabNameJ := 16: // ListContainers lists all containers using provided filter.
17: func (c *CLab) ListContainers(
18: 	ctx context.Context,
19: 	options ...ListOption,
20: ) ([]clabruntime.GenericContainer, error) {
21: 	opts := NewListOptions()
22: 
23: 	for _, opt := range options {
24: 		opt(opts)
25: 	}
26: 
27: 	var containers []clabruntime.GenericContainer
28:

30: 		ctrs, err := r.ListContainers(ctx, opts.ToFilters())
31: 		if err != nil {
32: 			return containers, fmt.Errorf("could not list containers: %v", err)
33: 		}
34:

36: 	}
37: 
38: 	return containers, nil
39: }

41: // ListNodesContainers lists all containers based on the nodes stored in clab instance.
42: func (c *CLab) ListNodesContainers(
43: 	ctx context.Context,
44: ) ([]clabruntime.GenericContainer, error) {
45: 	var containers []clabruntime.GenericContainer
46:

49: 		if err != nil {

63: // instance, ignoring errors for non found containers.

68: 
69: 	for _, n := range c.Nodes {
70: 		cts, err := n.GetContainers(ctx)
71: 		if err != nil {
72: 			continue
73: 		}
74: 
75: 		containers = append(containers, cts...)
76: 	}
77: 
78: 	return containers, nil
79: }
80: 
81: // ListContainerInterfaces list interfaces of the given container.
82: func (c *CLab) ListContainerInterfaces(
83: 	ctx context.Context,
84: 	container *clabruntime.GenericContainer,
85: ) (*clabtypes.ContainerInterfaces, error) {
86: 	containerInterfaces := clabtypes.ContainerInterfaces{}
87: 
88: 	if len(container.Names) > 0 {
89: 		containerInterfaces.ContainerName = container.Names[0]
90: 	}
91: 
92: 	// Retrieve the path to the container network NS
93: 	nodeNsPath, err := container.Runtime.GetNSPath(ctx, containerInterfaces.ContainerName)
94: 	if err != nil {
95: 		return nil, err
96: 	}
97: 
98: 	// Get network NS handle
99: 	var containerNsHandle netns.NsHandle
100: 
101: 	switch {
102: 	case nodeNsPath != "":
103: 		containerNsHandle, err = netns.GetFromPath(nodeNsPath)
104: 		if err != nil {
105: 			return nil, fmt.Errorf("unable to get container network NS handle: %w", err)
106: 		}
107: 		defer containerNsHandle.Close()
108: 	case container.Runtime.GetName() == "podman":
109: 		// Network NS path is empty and the runtime is Podman -> host network mode
110: 		// Manually get the handle for the root network namespace
111: 		containerNsHandle, err = netns.Get()
112: 		if err != nil {
113: 			return nil, fmt.Errorf("unable to get root network NS handle: %w", err)
114: 		}
115: 		defer containerNsHandle.Close()
116: 	default:
117: 		log.Warnf("Container %v has no namespace set, skipping!", containerInterfaces.ContainerName)
118: 		containerInterfaces.Interfaces = make([]*clabtypes.ContainerInterfaceDetails, 0)
119: 
120: 		return &containerInterfaces, nil
121: 	}
122: 
123: 	// Get Netlink handle in container network NS
124: 	netlinkHandle, err := netlink.NewHandleAt(containerNsHandle)
125: 	if err != nil {
126: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("unable to enter container network NS: %w", err)
127: 	}
128: 	defer netlinkHandle.Close()
129: 
130: 	interfaces, err := netlinkHandle.LinkList()
131: 	if err != nil {
132: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("unable to list network interfaces: %w", err)
133: 	}
134: 
135: 	containerInterfaces.Interfaces = make(
136: 		[]*clabtypes.ContainerInterfaceDetails,
137: 		0,
138: 		len(interfaces),
139: 	)
140: 
141: 	lab, node := TopoIdentity(container.Labels)
142: 
143: 	for _, iface := range interfaces {
144: 		reported, alias := reportedInterface(iface, lab, node)
145: 		attrs := reported.Attrs()
146: 
147: 		ifaceDetails := clabtypes.ContainerInterfaceDetails{
148: 			InterfaceName:  attrs.Name,
149: 			InterfaceAlias: alias,
150: 			InterfaceMTU:   attrs.MTU,
151: 			InterfaceMAC:   attrs.HardwareAddr.String(),
152: 			InterfaceIndex: attrs.Index,
153: 			InterfaceType:  reported.Type(),
154: 			InterfaceState: attrs.OperState.String(),
155: 		}
156: 		log.Debugf("Interface info: %+v", ifaceDetails)
157: 
158: 		containerInterfaces.Interfaces = append(containerInterfaces.Interfaces, &ifaceDetails)
159: 	}
160: 
161: 	log.Debugf("Fetched %v interfaces for %v", len(interfaces), containerInterfaces.ContainerName)
162: 
163: 	return &containerInterfaces, nil
164: }
165: 
166: // reportedInterface substitutes the host-side stitch interface for iface when
167: // one exists, keeping the topology name as alias so the swap is transparent.
168: func reportedInterface(iface netlink.Link, lab, node string) (netlink.Link, string) {
169: 	alias := iface.Attrs().Alias
170: 
171: 	topoName := alias
172: 	if topoName == "" {
173: 		topoName = iface.Attrs().Name
174: 	}
175: 
176: 	if tools, ok := clablinks.ToolsInterface(lab, node, topoName); ok {
177: 		return tools, topoName
178: 	}
179: 
180: 	return iface, alias
181: }

Wait — but in `listContainers`, `c.Nodes[ctr.Labels[clabconstants.RootNodeName]]` — c.Nodes is a map keyed by node short name (I think). Components are separate nodes created for each component. 74: func (n *sros) cleanupParkingNetNS() error {
75: 	name := clabutils.ParkingNetnsName(n.Cfg.LongName)
76: 	if _, err := clabutils.GetNamedNetNS(name); err != nil {
77: 		return nil
78: 	}
79: 
80: 	return netns.DeleteNamed(name)
81: }
82: 
83: // GetNSPath retrieves the Namespace Path.
84: func (n *sros) GetNSPath(ctx context.Context) (string, error) {
85: 	if n.isStandaloneNode() || (n.isDistributedCardNode() && n.rootCtrName == "") {
86: 		return n.DefaultNode.GetNSPath(ctx)
87: 	} else if n.isDistributedCardNode() {
88: 		return n.Runtime.GetNSPath(ctx, n.rootCtrName)
89: 	}
90: 	return n.netnsNode.GetNSPath(ctx)
91: }
92: 
93: // DeleteNetnsSymlink deletes the symlink file created for the container netns.
94: func (n *sros) DeleteNetnsSymlink() error {
95: 	var errs []error
96: 
97: 	// if it is the base node, then we need to delete the symlink for all the components.
98: 	if n.isDistributedBaseNode() {
99: 		for _, componentNode := range n.componentNodes {
100: 			errs = append(errs, componentNode.DeleteNetnsSymlink())
101: 		}
102: 		if n.netnsNode != nil {
103: 			errs = append(errs, n.netnsNode.DeleteNetnsSymlink())
104: 		}
105: 		errs = append(errs, clabutils.DeleteNetnsSymlink(n.Cfg.LongName))
106: 	}
107: 
108: 	errs = append(errs, n.DefaultNode.DeleteNetnsSymlink(), n.cleanupParkingNetNS())
109: 
110: 	return errors.Join(errs...)
111: }

Reasoning
So `c.Nodes` only includes topology-defined nodes (created via `NewNode`). Netns holders and component nodes are NOT in `c.Nodes` — they are stored on the node structs (`n.netnsNode`, `n.componentNodes`).

Wait — but then in the new `listContainers` code:

```go
containers = slices.DeleteFunc(containers, func(ctr clabruntime.GenericContainer) bool {
    _, isNode := c.Nodes[ctr.Labels[clabconstants.NodeName]]
    _, isComponent := c.Nodes[ctr.Labels[clabconstants.RootNodeName]]

    return !isNode && !isComponent
})
```

With `-t topo`, the list options filter by lab name first (labels include all lab containers: nodes, components, netns holders, tools like sshx, tailscale sidecars). Then:
- `isNode`: is the container's `clab-node-name` label a topology node name? For normal nodes yes. For components (e.g. "sr-a"), no — components are not in c.Nodes. For netns holder "sr-netns", no. For tools (sshx), the node name label is... tools containers: what labels do they carry? Let me check. Tools containers (sshx/gotty) — check their labels. Also tailscale sidecars — those are added via AddPlaceholderNode maybe, so they ARE in c.Nodes (see tailscale_test line 106: c.Nodes["n1-ts"] exists).

- `isComponent`: is the container's `clab-root-node-name` label a node in c.Nodes? For components and netns holders, RootNodeName = root short name, which IS in c.Nodes (if the root node is part of this topology). For tools containers, RootNodeName is empty → c.Nodes[""] miss → isComponent false; isNode for sshx tool: label clab-node-name? Tools containers probably have node-name label set to something. Let me check.

So the filter keeps: topology nodes' own containers, all components and netns holders of topology nodes (since RootNodeName is a topology node), and possibly tools if their node-name matches.

Hmm — but wait, there's a subtlety: the netns holder's NodeName is "sr-netns" and RootNodeName "sr". isComponent = c.Nodes["sr"] exists → kept. Good.

But wait — what about ext-containers? The comment says "containers that containerlab did not create (ie. ext-container) have no lab labels." Those are fetched via ListNodesContainersIgnoreNotFound and appended if ID not already present.

But there's a subtle issue: The label-based list with `WithListLabName(c.Config.Name)` — for `-t` case, they first list by lab name label. Then they delete anything that is neither a node nor a component of this topology. This would drop tools containers (sshx) that belong to the lab but... wait, would tools containers have RootNodeName? Let me check tools labels.

Actually, hold on — there's a bigger question: for `-t` case, does the previous behavior (base) list ALL containers of the lab (via ListNodesContainersIgnoreNotFound) or only node containers? In base, `-t` → ListNodesContainersIgnoreNotFound which lists containers by node names (the actual docker filter by long name). Components and netns holders are separate node objects... do `GetContainers` on the root sros node return all containers including components? Let me check `GetContainers` for sros distributed base node.

Actually in base, `inspect -t` showed components: the test file 09-srsim-pause-container.robot previously checked `ins -t ... -f json` yields `["clab-${lab-name}-sr14s-a"]` — only the CPM A component listed, meaning components appear and internal netns holder is hidden (because !o.Destroy.All filtered internal). So base behavior: `-t` lists containers by node (root node's GetContainers returns the CPM A container? hmm).

Let me check sros GetContainers.

Reasoning
Now I understand the flow:

- `srl.GetContainers`: for an SR Linux node with a netns holder, it returns the node's own container but with the holder's NetworkName/NetworkSettings/Ports (i.e., the node's management address as the holder owns it). This is the existing behavior.

- In the new `listContainers`:
  1. First, list all lab containers by label (`WithListLabName(c.Config.Name)`). That includes: node containers, components, netns holders, tools, etc.
  2. Delete containers where NodeName isn't in c.Nodes AND RootNodeName isn't in c.Nodes. This keeps components and holders (RootNodeName matches a real node). Tools containers: do they carry RootNodeName? Let me check what tool-type containers look like. If a tool container (sshx) has RootNodeName unset, NodeName unset (or something), then it would be deleted when `-t` is used. Hmm — that's a behavior change: `inspect -t` previously listed... actually in base, `-t` used `ListNodesContainersIgnoreNotFound` which lists containers for each node in c.Nodes — tools containers (sshx/gotty) are not in c.Nodes, so base `-t` did NOT list tools either. Wait — but actually the base `-t` path returns containers via node GetContainers. Does srl.GetContainers return both the node container AND the holder? Look at line 483-485: it returns only `cnts` (the node's container) with holder network settings. The holder itself is not returned. Hmm wait — `ListNodesContainersIgnoreNotFound` iterates over c.Nodes and calls GetContainers for each. For srl node: returns the node container with holder's IPs. So the holder isn't listed in base `-t` either. Actually wait, but in the robot test, `ins -t` yields exactly `["clab-...-sr14s-a"]` — only the CPM A. So components B and 1 are NOT listed via `-t` in base either! Because sros GetContainers for distributed base node returns only the CPM container (calcComponentName with cpmSlot).

  Hmm interesting. So in base, `inspect -t` on srsim showed only the CPM A container (with the mgmt IP forced from distNodeMgmtIPs). Components B, 1, and the holder were not shown. In head, `-t` now lists all containers with RootNodeName in c.Nodes: components B, 1, and netns holder sr-netns — then logicalNodeContainers collapses them to just `sr-a` (CPM A) with holder's network info. So the observable behavior for `-t` logical view matches base: only sr-a shown (test asserts `== ["clab-${lab-name}-sr14s-a"]` still passes).

  But with `--containers`, `-t` shows all components and the holder. With plain `inspect -t` (no flags) — logical view.

Now the dedup loop: after deleting non-node/non-component, it appends `nodeContainers` (from ListNodesContainersIgnoreNotFound) that aren't already in the list by ID. What containers does ListNodesContainersIgnoreNotFound return that aren't already in the label-based list? ext-containers (they have no lab labels so wouldn't match the lab-name filter... wait, ext-containers joined to the lab — do they carry the containerlab label? The comment says they don't). Also normal node containers would already be in the label list (dedup by ID). So the dedup loop's purpose: add ext-containers and any node containers not matching the label filter.

Hmm, but wait: does the label-based list with `WithListLabName(c.Config.Name)` return containers for the right lab? c.Config.Name is the lab name. OK.

Potential issue: `slices.ContainsFunc(containers, func(ctr) { return ctr.ID == nodeContainers[idx].ID })` — comparing ID; for ext-containers the ID should be set. Fine.

Now logicalNodeContainers: collapses containers into logical nodes.

Let me scrutinize logic:

Pass 1: build `holders` map: key = (lab, RootNodeName). For each container with RootNodeName != "":
- if InternalNode == "true" → holders[key] = &containers[idx]
- else if NodeName == key.node+"-a" → hasCPMA[key] = true

Wait, there's an issue: the switch `case key.node == "":` — skip. Then `case l[InternalNode] == "true": holders[key] = &containers[idx]`. Note: the holder overwrites any prior holder for the same key. Multiple holders per (lab, node)? Only one netns holder per node normally.

But hmm: the holders map value is a pointer to `containers[idx]` — taking address of slice element. That's fine as long as the slice isn't reallocated. It isn't.

Pass 2: for each container:
- name = NodeName label; key = (lab, RootNodeName)
- isCPM := name == key.node+"-a" || (name == key.node+"-b" && !hasCPMA[key])
- skip if ToolType != "" || InternalNode == "true" || (key.node != "" && !isCPM)
- if key.node == "" → key.node = name (so holder lookup key becomes (lab, name))
- if holder := holders[key]; holder != nil → copy NetworkName/NetworkSettings/Ports from holder.

Wait — there's a bug candidate here: for a standalone srl node (not distributed), the node container has no RootNodeName (it's a normal node). But it has a netns holder: the holder has RootNodeName = "srl" (the node's name) and InternalNode = true. So in pass 1: for the holder, key = (lab, "srl"), InternalNode → holders[(lab,"srl")] = holder.

In pass 2, for the srl node container: name = "srl", key.node = "" → key.node set to name → key = (lab, "srl"). holders[key] exists → copy holder's network settings. Result: "clab-lab1-srl 10.0.0.2" (holder's IP). Test asserts this. Good.

For distributed srsim: containers: sr-a (NodeName "sr-a", RootNodeName "sr"), sr-b, sr-1, sr-netns (InternalNode, RootNodeName "sr"). Pass 1: holder → holders[(lab1,"sr")] = netns; hasCPMA[(lab1,"sr")] = true (sr-a). Pass 2: sr-a: isCPM (name == "sr"+"-a") → true; skip? key.node = "sr" != "", isCPM true → not skipped. key.node != "" so key stays (lab1, "sr"). holders[(lab1,"sr")] → copy holder network. Good. sr-b: isCPM false (hasCPMA) → skipped (key.node != "" && !isCPM). sr-1: skipped. netns: InternalNode → skipped. Result: sr-a with holder's IP. Test wants "clab-lab1-sr-a 10.0.0.3". 

For lab2 (no CPM A): sr-b: pass 1: hasCPMA[(lab2,"sr")] = false (sr-b name == "sr-b", checked only in the `case l[NodeName] == key.node+"-a"` branch — not matched). Pass 2: isCPM = name=="sr-a" (no) || (name=="sr-b" && !hasCPMA) → true. So sr-b listed with holder IP 10.0.0.4. Test wants "clab-lab2-sr-b 10.0.0.4". Good.

Now, the interesting logic detail: the "CPM A/B" naming assumption: components are named <root>-<lowercase slot>. For slot "A" → "sr-a". For card slots "1", "2" → "sr-1". So the isCPM check: name == root+"-a" or root+"-b" (when no a). What about a component slot "b" when CPM A exists — skipped. What if a node is named such that a *card* is named "sr-b" without a CPM A? Then it's treated as the CPM. Edge case: distributed sros always has CPM A? By topology definition, a distributed srsim must have components with slot A (CPM)? Probably required. The test 15 includes slot A and B. Fine.

Potential correctness issue: `holders[key]` when key.node == "" in pass 2 — set to name; but the holder for a normal node has RootNodeName = node name — matches. Good.

But what about a normal node named e.g. "srl" whose holder is found, and ALSO another container in the same lab with RootNodeName="srl"? Only holder. OK.

Now, an important behavior change: deploy summary. In deploy.go, the summary table previously included internal containers when...? Let me check base deploy.go: it called `PrintContainerInspect(result.Containers, o)` and PrintContainerInspect filtered internal nodes only when !o.Destroy.All. Deploy has o.Destroy.All false presumably, so internal was filtered. Now deploy filters internal containers before printing, unconditionally. Same effect. But now PrintContainerInspect no longer filters — check other callers of PrintContainerInspect.

Reasoning
So `deploy` returns `c.ListNodesContainers(ctx)` — iterating over `c.Nodes` (topology nodes only) calling `GetContainers`. For srsim distributed node, sros.GetContainers returns only the CPM A container. So result.Containers = [linux, sr-a(with forced mgmt IP)]. Good — so deploy summary shows "linux sr-a" as the test expects. And the internal filter in deploy.go... wait — result.Containers from ListNodesContainers wouldn't contain netns holders anyway (srl.GetContainers returns only the node's own container with holder's IPs; sros returns CPM container). Hmm, but what about a case where the node container is not found and only the netns holder exists (srl.GetContainers returns netnsNode.GetContainers on ErrContainersNotFound — that returns the holder container, labeled InternalNode=true). That's why the internal filter in deploy.go still matters: when a node container is missing, the holder may appear in result.Containers with the InternalNode label, and the deploy summary shouldn't list it. OK, so the deploy.go change is a faithful preservation of behavior.

Wait, one subtlety: in base, `PrintContainerInspect` filtered internal only when `!o.Destroy.All`. In deploy, o.Destroy.All is presumably always false (no --all flag on deploy). Let me verify deploy cmd doesn't register Destroy.All. grep for `Destroy.All` in cmd.

Reasoning
Now let me think about potential issues in the new `listContainers`:

**Issue A: `--topo` with ext-containers / node filtering semantics.** With `-t`, list options = `WithListLabName(c.Config.Name)`. Wait — c.Config.Name for a `-t` inspect: NewContainerLab parses the topology so Config.Name is the lab name. OK.

Then delete non-node/non-component. Then add node containers not already present. Consider a **host-network node** (e.g. `network-mode: host`) or a node without the containerlab label... they'd be fetched by ListNodesContainersIgnoreNotFound and appended (they have no lab label so weren't in the label list). Fine.

Consider **ext-containers**: they are in c.Nodes as placeholder nodes. ListNodesContainersIgnoreNotFound returns them; label list doesn't include them (no containerlab label? Actually ext-containers might have the containerlab label set? The comment says no). They're appended. Good.

Now consider a subtle case: two labs, `-t topo-of-lab-A`. Label list = lab A containers. DeleteFunc keeps containers whose NodeName ∈ c.Nodes or RootNodeName ∈ c.Nodes. But what if a container from another lab has the same node name? Not possible with clab- prefix naming... Long names are `clab-<lab>-<node>`, and NodeName label is short name. A container from lab B named "linux"? Docker container names are unique; different labs have different long names. But the label list filtered by lab name already excludes other labs. Fine.

**Issue B: tools containers filtered out with `-t`.** Tools (sshx/gotty) containers belong to the lab. In base, `-t` didn't list them (ListNodesContainersIgnoreNotFound doesn't include tools). But `inspect --name <lab>` (base) listed by label — includes tools (they have containerlab label). Now in head, with `--name` or `--all`, tools containers pass through listContainers, then get collapsed by `logicalNodeContainers` — which skips ToolType != "". So tools are hidden by default in the logical view (same as base, since base only hid internal, not tools... wait!). 

Base behavior for `inspect --all`: `if !o.Destroy.All { hide internal }` — with --all, internal was SHOWN, and tools were shown. Head behavior: with `--all` (no --containers), tools and internal and components are all hidden/collapsed. That's the intended new behavior per docs and the updated test (09-srsim-pause-container.robot now asserts that with `--all -f json`, the holder is NOT contained — changed from before where --all showed it). OK, intended.

**Issue C: `--name <lab>` + `--containers`**: list by lab name label → all lab containers including tools, components, holders. No filtering. Good.

**Issue D: `--all` + `--containers`**: all clab-labeled containers across all labs. Fine.

**Issue E: `--all` without --containers: logicalNodeContainers over all labs.** Test asserts. Fine.

**Issue F: In the `-t` branch, components/holders of the lab are kept by the RootNodeName check — but what about *another lab's* node named same as one of our nodes?** Filtered by lab name already.

**Issue G: What about `inspect -t` on a topology whose nodes' containers don't have the containerlab label?** e.g. ext-containers — handled.

**Issue H: Duplicate `node-name` in different... covered by test.

**Issue I: In pass 2 of logicalNodeContainers, `key.node != "" && !isCPM` skips containers with RootNodeName set that aren't CPM-a/b. But what about the case where a node has RootNodeName label pointing to itself? Not set for normal nodes.

**Issue J: Pointer into slice + copying `ctr := containers[idx]` then modifying ctr fields — fine.

**Issue K: `hasCPMA` computed in pass 1 only checks `l[NodeName] == key.node+"-a"` in the switch — but only reached when `key.node != ""` and not internal. What if the CPM A container is internal? It isn't.

Now the **isCPM heuristic**: "Components are named <root node>-<lowercase slot>". What if a *regular node* in the lab has a RootNodeName label? Tools like sshx — do they have RootNodeName? Let me check what labels tool containers get. If a tool container (sshx) has NodeName = "sshx" or none and RootNodeName unset, pass 2: key.node == "" → key.node = name. But ToolType != "" → skip. OK.

What about tailscale sidecars? They are added via AddPlaceholderNode with name "n1-ts"; labels? Let me check tailscale sidecar labels — they might have RootNodeName? Let me check nodes/tailscale.

22: func (c *CLab) runtimeNodeGroups(
23: 	ctx context.Context,
24: ) (map[string]*runtimeNodeGroup, error) {
25: 	containers, err := c.ListContainers(ctx, WithListLabName(c.Config.Name))
26: 	if err != nil {
27: 		return nil, err
28: 	}
29: 
30: 	result := make(map[string]*runtimeNodeGroup)
31: 	var duplicates []string
32: 
33: 	for _, ctr := range containers {
34: 		if ctr.Labels[clabconstants.ToolType] != "" {
35: 			continue
36: 		}
37: 
38: 		nodeName := ctr.Labels[clabconstants.NodeName]
39: 		if nodeName == "" {
40: 			continue
41: 		}
42: 
43: 		groupName := ctr.Labels[clabconstants.RootNodeName]
44: 		distributed := groupName != ""
45: 		if groupName == "" {
46: 			groupName = nodeName
47: 		}
48: 
49: 		group, exists := result[groupName]
50: 		if !exists {
51: 			group = &runtimeNodeGroup{
52: 				name:        groupName,
53: 				distributed: distributed,
54: 			}
55: 			result[groupName] = group
56: 		}
57: 		group.containers = append(group.containers, ctr)
58: 		group.distributed = group.distributed || distributed
59: 
60: 		if !group.distributed && len(group.containers) > 1 {
61: 			duplicates = append(duplicates, nodeName)
62: 			continue
63: 		}
64: 	}
65: 
66: 	// Pre-existing resources have no containerlab labels, but still participate in apply.
67: 	for nodeName, node := range c.Nodes {
68: 		cfg := node.Config()
69: 		if cfg == nil {
70: 			continue
71: 		}
72: 		if _, exists := result[nodeName]; exists {
73: 			continue
74: 		}
75: 		if cfg.IsRootNamespaceBased {
76: 			result[nodeName] = &runtimeNodeGroup{
77: 				name:               nodeName,
78: 				rootNamespaceBased: true,
79: 			}
80: 			continue
81: 		}
82: 		if !cfg.SkipUniquenessCheck || node.GetContainerStatus(ctx) == clabruntime.NotFound {
83: 			continue
84: 		}
85: 		result[nodeName] = &runtimeNodeGroup{name: nodeName, external: true}
86: 	}
87:

89: 		sort.Strings(duplicates)
90: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf(
91: 			"apply supports only single-container nodes, found multiple containers for nodes: %s",
92: 			strings.Join(duplicates, ", "),
93: 		)
94: 	}
95:

97: 		sort.Slice(group.containers, func(i, j int) bool {
98: 			return runtimeContainerLess(group.containers[i], group.containers[j])
99: 		})
100: 	}
101: 
102: 	return result, nil
103: }

105: // NeedsInitialDeploy reports whether the lab has no runtime state yet, i.e. whether

110: 		if !ok {

132: func (c *CLab) needsInitialDeploy(currentNodes map[string]*runtimeNodeGroup) (bool, error) {
133: 	if len(currentNodes) == 0 {
134: 		return true, nil
135: 	}

138: 			return false, nil
139: 		}

143: 	return state == nil, err

146: func (c *CLab) setMgmtBridgeFromRuntime(
147: 	currentNodes map[string]*runtimeNodeGroup,
148: ) error {
149: 	if c.Config.Mgmt.Bridge != "" {
150: 		return nil
151: 	}
152: 
153: 	var bridge string
154: 	for _, runtimeNode := range currentNodes {
155: 		for _, ctr := range runtimeNode.containers {
156: 			// only containers attached to the default management network, or
157: 			// without a runtime network, carry the default network's bridge
158: 			// label. Unknown networks, e.g. ones dropped from the mgmt list,
159: 			// must not contribute their bridge.
160: 			switch ctr.NetworkName {
161: 			case "", c.Config.Mgmt.Network:
162: 			default:
163: 				continue
164: 			}
165: 			ctrBridge := ctr.Labels[clabconstants.NodeMgmtNetBr]
166: 			if ctrBridge == "" {
167: 				continue
168: 			}
169: 			if bridge == "" {
170: 				bridge = ctrBridge
171: 				continue
172: 			}
173: 			if bridge != ctrBridge {
174: 				return fmt.Errorf(
175: 					"runtime lab has conflicting management bridge labels: %q and %q",
176: 					bridge,
177: 					ctrBridge,
178: 				)
179: 			}
180: 		}
181: 	}
182: 
183: 	if bridge != "" {
184: 		c.Config.Mgmt.Bridge = bridge
185: 	}
186: 
187: 	return nil
188: }

190: func (c *CLab) updateRuntimeInfoForExistingNodes(ctx context.Context) error {
191: 	for _, nodeName := range sortedNodeNames(c.Nodes) {
192: 		node := c.Nodes[nodeName]
193: 		if node.GetContainerStatus(ctx) == clabruntime.NotFound {
194: 			continue
195: 		}
196: 		if err := node.UpdateConfigWithRuntimeInfo(ctx); err != nil {
197: 			return fmt.Errorf("failed to update runtime info for node %q: %w", nodeName, err)
198: 		}
199: 	}
200: 
201: 	return nil
202: }

204: func sortedRuntimeNodeGroupNames(nodes map[string]*runtimeNodeGroup) []string {
205: 	names := make([]string, 0, len(nodes))
206: 	for name := range nodes {
207: 		names = append(names, name)
208: 	}
209: 	sort.Strings(names)
210: 	return names
211: }

213: func runtimeContainerNodeName(ctr clabruntime.GenericContainer) string {
214: 	if name := ctr.Labels[clabconstants.NodeName]; name != "" {
215: 		return name
216: 	}
217: 	if len(ctr.Names) > 0 {
218: 		return ctr.Names[0]
219: 	}
220: 	return ctr.ID
221: }
222: 
223: func runtimeContainerLess(a, b clabruntime.GenericContainer) bool {
224: 	// internal holders own the network namespace of the other containers in the group,
225: 	// so they sort first to be deleted last.
226: 	aInternal := a.Labels[clabconstants.InternalNode] == "true"
227: 	bInternal := b.Labels[clabconstants.InternalNode] == "true"
228: 	if aInternal != bInternal {
229: 		return aInternal
230: 	}
231: 
232: 	aName := runtimeContainerNodeName(a)
233: 	bName := runtimeContainerNodeName(b)
234: 
235: 	aOrder := runtimeComponentSortOrder(aName)
236: 	bOrder := runtimeComponentSortOrder(bName)
237: 	if aOrder != bOrder {
238: 		return aOrder < bOrder
239: 	}
240: 
241: 	return aName < bName
242: }
243: 
244: func runtimeComponentSortOrder(name string) int {
245: 	idx := strings.LastIndex(name, "-")
246: 	if idx < 0 || idx == len(name)-1 {
247: 		return 0
248: 	}
249: 
250: 	slot := strings.ToUpper(name[idx+1:])
251: 	if len(slot) == 1 && slot[0] >= 'A' && slot[0] <= 'Z' {
252: 		return 100 - int(slot[0])
253: 	}
254: 
255: 	var n int
256: 	for _, r := range slot {
257: 		if r < '0' || r > '9' {
258: 			return 0
259: 		}
260: 		n = n*10 + int(r-'0')
261: 	}
262: 
263: 	return n
264: }

(End of file - total 264 lines)

So tools being excluded is documented-ish ("only the containers of the nodes defined in that topology file"). sshx is not in the topo file. But in base, `-t` didn't list tools either. Consistent.

Hmm wait, actually — is that right? Let me double check base behavior of `-t`: `ListNodesContainersIgnoreNotFound` iterates `c.Nodes`. Tools are not in c.Nodes → excluded. Yes.

3. **Big one — the `-t` branch in head: what if the topology file selects a lab that's not deployed?** `WithListLabName` returns nothing; then ListNodesContainersIgnoreNotFound returns nothing → empty. Base: same.

4. **`inspect --topo` where a node in the topology has an `ext-container` kind**: c.Nodes includes placeholder ext-container nodes. Their GetContainers returns the ext container. In label list: ext containers have no containerlab label → not in label list. Appended. Good.

But wait — one interesting interaction: For ext-container nodes, in logicalNodeContainers pass 2, they have labels: NodeName? ext-containers get labels? Probably NodeName set (addDefaultLabels). key.node == "" → key.node = name. holders lookup fails. Result: ext container included. Good.

5. **Now a REAL potential bug: `isComponent := c.Nodes[ctr.Labels[clabconstants.RootNodeName]]` keeps containers whose RootNodeName matches a topology node name — but what about containers in the same lab belonging to another lab's... no.

What about *tools* containers attached to the lab in the `-t` case — dropped. And what about *tailscale sidecars*? kept (they're in c.Nodes), then hidden in logical view via InternalNode, but SHOWN in `--containers`/`--details` view with `-t`. In base `-t --details`: ListNodesContainersIgnoreNotFound returns sidecars (they're c.Nodes nodes) and internal filter: `if !o.Destroy.All { hide internal }` — so with `--details` (and !all), base HID the sidecar. In head with `-t --details`, the sidecar IS shown (since --details returns everything kept by the DeleteFunc). Behavior change for `-t --details`: now shows internal sidecars/holders. Hmm, is that intended? The docs for --details: "With this flag inspect command will output every bit of information about the running containers." Previously `-t --details` showed every bit too? Let me check base: `-t` → ListNodesContainersIgnoreNotFound → includes srl node container (with holder's network info merged) but NOT the holder itself; then internal filter hides internal (sidecar, holder-if-node-missing) when !all. So base `-t --details` showed sidecars? No wait: ListNodesContainersIgnoreNotFound DOES include sidecars because sidecars are in c.Nodes (AddPlaceholderNode). Then internal filter hid them when !Destroy.All. So base `-t --details` hid sidecars and holders. Head `-t --details` shows them (DeleteFunc keeps them since they're in c.Nodes; then --details returns unfiltered). This is the new intended behavior: "--details always describes every container of a lab" per the comment. And the updated test 09 confirms: `--details` now contains the netns holder ("Should Contain ... sr14s-netns" when format is `--details`). Yes, the test was changed accordingly: IF $format == '-f json' → Not Contain; ELSE (--details) → Contain. So intended.

6. **Now, `logicalNodeContainers` and the `-t` case for a lab NOT matching the topo's nodes** — covered.

7. **Now the `--containers` flag name collision**: `c.Flags().BoolVarP(&o.Inspect.Containers, "containers", "", ...)`. Also inspectCmd registers `o.Destroy.All` as `--all`. No conflicts.

8. **inspect_test.go**: uses `logicalNodeContainers`. The test: `tool := ctr("lab1", "", "", false, "10.0.0.9"); tool.Labels[ToolType] = "sshx"`. Note the helper sets `Labels[NodeName] = name` even when name is "" — so the tool has NodeName label "" and Containerlab "lab1". OK.

Order matters: the test relies on input order producing want order. got rows: linux(1st non-skipped?) Let's trace: containers order: tool, tailscale(internal, no root), linux, srl, srl-netns(holder, root=srl), sr-1(root=sr), sr-b(root=sr), sr-a(root=sr), sr-netns(root=sr, internal), lab2 sr-1, sr-b, sr-netns, linux.

- tool: key.node = "" → skip.

want matches. Good, the unit test covers the core logic.

9. **Naming assumption risk**: `isCPM := name == key.node+"-a" || (name == key.node+"-b" && !hasCPMA[key])`. What if a *card* component has slot "A"? Cards use numeric slots ("1","2"), CPM slots are "A"/"B". What if user defines components with lowercase slots? calcComponentName lowercases? Let me check calcComponentName.
